for some constantϑ>0\vartheta>0. Ifκ−⩾0\kappa_{-}\geqslant 0, thenw=yw=y, so for everyy∈𝒞Ny\in\mathcal{C}_{N}, we haveVar​(w)⩾ε⩾ε/2\mathrm{Var}(w)\geqslant\varepsilon\geqslant\varepsilon/2. Hence the constraint in (57) is automatically satisfied, and (61) follows.

Consequently,𝒞N⊆SN\mathcal{C}_{N}\subseteq S_{N}, whereSNS_{N}is as defined in Proposition5.4. Under the mean-field condition (4), Proposition5.4yieldsℙ​(b​(X)∈𝒞N)=ℙ​(∑i(bi​(X)−b¯​(X))2⩾ε​N)→0as​N→∞,\mathbb{P}\bigl(b(X)\in\mathcal{C}_{N}\bigr)=\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\sum_{i}\bigl(b_{i}(X)-\bar{b}(X)\bigr)^{2}\geqslant\varepsilon N\Bigr)\to 0\quad\text{as }N\to\infty,(62)

which holds for any fixedε>0\varepsilon>0.
Recall that|mi​(x)|⩽γ​κ2|m_{i}(x)|\leqslant\gamma\kappa^{2}. SinceΛ′′\Lambda^{\prime\prime}is continuous and strictly positive, setc∗=inf|t|⩽γ​κ2Λ′′​(β0​t+h0)>0.c_{*}=\inf_{|t|\leqslant\gamma\kappa^{2}}\Lambda^{\prime\prime}(\beta_{0}t+h_{0})>0.Then by the mean value theorem, for anyx∈[κ−,κ+]Nx\in[\kappa_{-},\kappa_{+}]^{N},|bi​(x)−bj​(x)|=|Λ′​(β0​mi​(x)+h0)−Λ′​(β0​mj​(x)+h0)|⩾β0​c∗​|mi​(x)−mj​(x)|.|b_{i}(x)-b_{j}(x)|=\big|\Lambda^{\prime}(\beta_{0}m_{i}(x)+h_{0})-\Lambda^{\prime}(\beta_{0}m_{j}(x)+h_{0})\big|\geqslant\beta_{0}c_{*}|m_{i}(x)-m_{j}(x)|.

Hence,∑i(mi​(X)−m¯​(X))2⩽(β0​c∗)−2​∑i(bi​(X)−b¯​(X))2\sum_{i}\bigl(m_{i}(X)-\bar{m}(X)\bigr)^{2}\leqslant(\beta_{0}c_{*})^{-2}\sum_{i}\bigl(b_{i}(X)-\bar{b}(X)\bigr)^{2}. Therefore, by (62),TN​(X)=op​(1),T_{N}(X)=o_{p}(1),as desired.

## Appendix AProof of Theorem1.3

In this section, we prove Theorem1.3. The pseudo-likelihood function is strongly concave wheneverTN​(X)>0T_{N}(X)>0, and by hypothesis this occurs with probability tending to one. Hence, to establish the existence of the MPLE it suffices to show that, with probability tending to one,lim|β|+|h|→∞L​(β,h|X)=−∞.\lim_{\left|\beta\right|+\left|h\right|\to\infty}L(\beta,h|X)=-\infty.(63)

We first state the following sufficient condition for (63), which is of similar nature as in[CSW24, Lemma 2.3].

## Lemma A.1.

Letx∈[κ−,κ+]Nx\in[\kappa_{-},\kappa_{+}]^{N}such thatI​(xi)<∞I(x_{i})<\inftyfor eachiiand assume that there exist distinct1⩽i,j,k,ℓ⩽N1\leqslant i,j,k,\ell\leqslant Nanda∈ℝa\in\mathbb{R}such thatxi≠κ−,mi​(x)>a,xj≠κ+,mj​(x)>a,xk≠κ−,mk​(x)<a,xl≠κ+,ml​(x)<a.\displaystyle\begin{split}&x_{i}\neq\kappa_{-},m_{i}(x)>a,\,\,x_{j}\neq\kappa_{+},m_{j}(x)>a,\\
&x_{k}\neq\kappa_{-},m_{k}(x)<a,\,\,x_{l}\neq\kappa_{+},m_{l}(x)<a.\end{split}(64)

Thenlim|β|+|h|→∞L​(β,h|x)=−∞.\lim_{|\beta|+|h|\to\infty}L(\beta,h|x)=-\infty.

## Proof.

Recall the log pseudo-likelihood function,L​(β,h|x)=∑ixi​(β​mi​(x)+h)−Λ​(β​mi​(x)+h)=∑iT​(β​mi​(x)+h,xi),L(\beta,h|x)=\sum_{i}x_{i}(\beta m_{i}(x)+h)-\Lambda(\beta m_{i}(x)+h)=\sum_{i}T(\beta m_{i}(x)+h,x_{i}),

whereΛ​(λ)=log​∫κ−κ+exp⁡(λ​z)​𝑑μ​(z)\Lambda(\lambda)=\log\int_{\kappa_{-}}^{\kappa_{+}}\exp(\lambda z)d\mu(z)for the reference measureμ\mu, andT​(λ,x):=x​λ−Λ​(λ)T(\lambda,x):=x\lambda-\Lambda(\lambda)forλ∈ℝ,x∈[κ−,κ+]\lambda\in\mathbb{R},x\in[\kappa_{-},\kappa_{+}]. Then we haveT​(λ,x)⩽supλ∈ℝT​(λ,x)=I​(x),T(\lambda,x)\leqslant\sup_{\lambda\in\mathbb{R}}T(\lambda,x)=I(x),where the equality part is due to the fact in Definition1.1. Thus, ifI​(x)<∞I(x)<\infty, then the mapλ↦Ti​(λ,x)\lambda\mapsto T_{i}(\lambda,x)is bounded above.

We now claim thatlimλ→∞T​(λ,x)=−∞​if​x<κ+,limλ→−∞T​(λ,x)=−∞​if​x>κ−.\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}T(\lambda,x)=-\infty\text{ if }x<\kappa_{+},\quad\lim_{\lambda\to-\infty}T(\lambda,x)=-\infty\text{ if }x>\kappa_{-}.(65)

To see the claim, note thatκ+∈supp​(μ)\kappa_{+}\in\text{supp}(\mu), thusμ​([κ+−δ,κ+])>0\mu([\kappa_{+}-\delta,\kappa_{+}])>0for everyδ>0\delta>0. Forλ⩾0\lambda\geqslant 0, we haveΛ​(λ)\displaystyle\Lambda(\lambda)=log⁡(∫κ−κ+eλ​z​𝑑μ​(z))⩾log⁡(∫κ+−δκ+eλ​z​𝑑μ​(z))\displaystyle=\log\Big(\int_{\kappa_{-}}^{\kappa_{+}}e^{\lambda z}d\mu(z)\Big)\geqslant\log\Big(\int_{\kappa_{+}-\delta}^{\kappa_{+}}e^{\lambda z}d\mu(z)\Big)⩾log⁡(eλ​(κ+−δ)⋅μ​([κ+−δ,κ+]))=λ​(κ+−δ)+log⁡(μ​([κ+−δ,κ+])).\displaystyle\geqslant\log\Big(e^{\lambda(\kappa_{+}-\delta)}\cdot\mu([\kappa_{+}-\delta,\kappa_{+}])\Big)=\lambda(\kappa_{+}-\delta)+\log(\mu([\kappa_{+}-\delta,\kappa_{+}])).

Forx∈(κ−,κ+)x\in(\kappa_{-},\kappa_{+}),
takingδ=κ+−x2>0\delta=\frac{\kappa_{+}-x}{2}>0, we havex​λ−Λ​(λ)⩽(x−κ+)​λ2−log⁡(μ​([κ+−δ,κ+])).\displaystyle x\lambda-\Lambda(\lambda)\leqslant\frac{(x-\kappa_{+})\lambda}{2}-\log(\mu([\kappa_{+}-\delta,\kappa_{+}])).

Asλ→+∞\lambda\to+\infty, it is clear thatx​λ−Λ​(λ)→−∞x\lambda-\Lambda(\lambda)\to-\inftysincex−κ+<0x-\kappa_{+}<0. The argument for the casex>κ−x>\kappa_{-}is similar.

SinceI​(xr)<∞I(x_{r})<\inftyfor allr∈[N]r\in[N]by assumption, it suffices to show thatlim|β|+|h|→∞T​(β​ms​(x)+h,xs)=−∞\lim_{\left|\beta\right|+\left|h\right|\to\infty}T(\beta m_{s}(x)+h,x_{s})=-\infty

for somes∈{i,j,k,ℓ}s\in\{i,j,k,\ell\}where the indicesi,j,k,ℓi,j,k,\ellare as in (64).
We argue by contradiction. Suppose fori,j,k,ℓi,j,k,\ellsatisfying (64), we haveT​(β​ms​(x)+h,xs)⩾−K​fors∈{i,j,k,ℓ},T(\beta m_{s}(x)+h,x_{s})\geqslant-K\ \text{for $s\in\{i,j,k,\ell\}$},for a common subsequence(β,h)(\beta,h)satisfying|β|+|h|→∞|\beta|+|h|\to\infty.
By slight abuse of notation, we are going to restrict to this common subsequence. By (65), sincexj,xℓ≠κ+x_{j},x_{\ell}\neq\kappa_{+}, andxi,xk≠κ−x_{i},x_{k}\neq\kappa-then there exists a constantK1K_{1}(depending only onK,μ,xi,xj,xk,xℓK,\mu,x_{i},x_{j},x_{k},x_{\ell}) such thatβ​mj​(x)+h⩽K1\displaystyle\beta m_{j}(x)+h\leqslant K_{1}\quadandβ​mℓ​(x)+h⩽K1,\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\beta m_{\ell}(x)+h\leqslant K_{1},β​mi​(x)+h⩾−K1\displaystyle\beta m_{i}(x)+h\geqslant-K_{1}\quadandβ​mk​(x)+h⩾−K1.\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\beta m_{k}(x)+h\geqslant-K_{1}.

This confineshhto the interval:max⁡(−β​mi​(x)−K1,−β​mk​(x)−K1)⩽h⩽min⁡(−β​mj​(x)+K1,−β​ml​(x)+K1).\max(-\beta m_{i}(x)-K_{1},-\beta m_{k}(x)-K_{1})\leqslant h\leqslant\min(-\beta m_{j}(x)+K_{1},-\beta m_{l}(x)+K_{1}).(66)

For this interval to be non-empty, its upper bound must be greater than its lower bound.

Caseβ⩾0\beta\geqslant 0: The conditionsmj​(x)>a>mk​(x)m_{j}(x)>a>m_{k}(x)implymj​(x)>mk​(x)m_{j}(x)>m_{k}(x). The non-empty interval constraint implies−β​mk​(x)−K1<−β​mj​(x)+K1-\beta m_{k}(x)-K_{1}<-\beta m_{j}(x)+K_{1}, which simplifies toβ​(mj​(x)−mk​(x))<2​K1\beta(m_{j}(x)-m_{k}(x))<2K_{1}. Therefore, we haveβ<2​K1/(mj​(x)−mk​(x)).\beta<2K_{1}/(m_{j}(x)-m_{k}(x)).(67)

Caseβ<0\beta<0: The conditionsmi​(x)>a>ml​(x)m_{i}(x)>a>m_{l}(x)implymi​(x)>ml​(x)m_{i}(x)>m_{l}(x). The constraint implies−β​mi​(x)−K1<−β​ml​(x)+K~-\beta m_{i}(x)-K_{1}<-\beta m_{l}(x)+\tilde{K}, which simplifies to−β​(mi​(x)−ml​(x))<2​K1-\beta(m_{i}(x)-m_{l}(x))<2K_{1}.
Therefore, we have−β<2​K1/(mi​(x)−ml​(x)).-\beta<2K_{1}/(m_{i}(x)-m_{l}(x)).(68)

Combining (67) and (68), we obtain|β|<max⁡(2​K1mj​(x)−mk​(x),2​K1mi​(x)−ml​(x)),|\beta|<\max\Big(\frac{2K_{1}}{m_{j}(x)-m_{k}(x)},\frac{2K_{1}}{m_{i}(x)-m_{l}(x)}\Big),

which, in combination with (66) forces|h||h|to be bounded. So,|β|+|h||\beta|+|h|can not be arbitrarily large. This concludes the proof of the lemma.■\blacksquare

In proving existence, the main difficulty is verifying condition (64) under the assumptions of Theorem1.3. To that end, we introduce the following lemma, which quantifies the idea that if a bounded sequence exhibits nontrivial variability, then a linear fraction of its entries are separated by a fixed gap.

there exists an integerrrwith|r|⩽K|r|\leqslant Kand−γ​κ2⩽(r−1)​ζ<r​ζ⩽γ​κ2-\gamma\kappa^{2}\leqslant(r-1)\zeta<r\zeta\leqslant\gamma\kappa^{2}such that|{i∈[N]:mi∈[−γ​κ2,(r−1)​ζ]}|⩾ε​N​and​|{i∈[N]:mi∈[r​ζ,γ​κ2]}|⩾ε​N.\displaystyle|\{i\in[N]:m_{i}\in[-\gamma\kappa^{2},(r-1)\zeta]\}|\geqslant\varepsilon N\text{ and }|\{i\in[N]:m_{i}\in[r\zeta,\gamma\kappa^{2}]\}|\geqslant\varepsilon N.

Definer=max⁡{s∈ℤ:|{i∈[N]:mi∈[s​ζ,γ​κ2]}|⩾ε​N}.r=\max\left\{s\in\mathbb{Z}:\left|\left\{i\in[N]:m_{i}\in[s\zeta,\gamma\kappa^{2}]\right\}\right|\geqslant\varepsilon N\right\}.This set is nonempty because for integers⩽−γ​κ2/ζs\leqslant-\gamma\kappa^{2}/\zetawe have[s​ζ,γ​κ2]⊇[−γ​κ2,γ​κ2][s\zeta,\gamma\kappa^{2}]\supseteq[-\gamma\kappa^{2},\gamma\kappa^{2}], hence the count isN⩾ε​NN\geqslant\varepsilon N. In either of the cases we have that at most2​ε​N2\varepsilon Nof themim_{i}can lie in the interval[−γ​κ2,γ​κ2]∖[(r−1)​ζ,(r+1)​ζ][-\gamma\kappa^{2},\gamma\kappa^{2}]\setminus[(r-1)\zeta,(r+1)\zeta], because by maximality ofrrwe have|{i:mi∈[(r+1)​ζ,γ​κ2]}|<ε​N.|\{i:m_{i}\in[(r+1)\zeta,\gamma\kappa^{2}]\}|<\varepsilon N.We now derive an upper bound on the left-hand side of (70) by considering the following cases.

By the choice ofε\varepsilonandδ\delta, the sum of these contributions is at mostδ​N2/2\delta N^{2}/2, which contradicts (70). Therefore,|{i∈[N]:mi⩽(r−1)​ζ}|⩾ε​N\left|\left\{i\in[N]:m_{i}\leqslant(r-1)\zeta\right\}\right|\geqslant\varepsilon Nand the lemma follows.

## Proof of Theorem1.3: Existence of MPLE.

By LemmaA.2, with probability tending to one,∃integer​r∈[−K,K]​such that​|Ir​ζ,γ​κ2|⩾ε​N​and​|I−γ​κ2,(r−1)​ζ|⩾ε​N.\exists\text{ integer }r\in[-K,K]\text{ such that }|I_{r\zeta,\gamma\kappa^{2}}|\geqslant\varepsilon N\text{ and }|I_{-\gamma\kappa^{2},(r-1)\zeta}|\geqslant\varepsilon N.(71)

Once the claim is established, we can combine with (71) to deduce that
with probability tending to one, there exists an integerr∈[−K,K]r\in[-K,K]such that|I(r−1/2)​ζ,γ​κ2|⩾|Ir​ζ,γ​κ2|⩾ε​N⩾2,\displaystyle|I_{(r-1/2)\zeta,\gamma\kappa^{2}}|\geqslant|I_{r\zeta,\gamma\kappa^{2}}|\geqslant\varepsilon N\geqslant 2,and{Xi:i∈I(r−1/2)​ζ,γ​κ2}≠{κ−},{κ+},\displaystyle\ \{X_{i}:i\in I_{(r-1/2)\zeta,\gamma\kappa^{2}}\}\neq\{\kappa_{-}\},\{\kappa_{+}\},|I−γ​κ2,(r−1/2)​ζ|⩾|I−γ​κ2,(r−1)​ζ|⩾ε​N⩾2,\displaystyle|I_{-\gamma\kappa^{2},(r-1/2)\zeta}|\geqslant|I_{-\gamma\kappa^{2},(r-1)\zeta}|\geqslant\varepsilon N\geqslant 2,and{Xi:i∈I−γ​κ2,(r−1/2)​ζ}≠{κ−},{κ+}.\displaystyle\ \{X_{i}:i\in I_{-\gamma\kappa^{2},(r-1/2)\zeta}\}\neq\{\kappa_{-}\},\{\kappa_{+}\}.

## Appendix BProofs for Applications

In this section we present the proofs of the main results in Section2. We repeatedly use
Lemmas3.4(b) and3.5in the following form. To verify the mean-field condition (4),
it suffices to check Assumption1.2, the nontriviality condition (7),
the strong pseudo-regularity condition (6), andTr⁡(ℛ2)=o​(N/log⁡N)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathcal{R}^{2})=o(N/\log N), and then apply Lemma3.4(b). When Theorem1.13is applied, we also need
the spectral-gap condition (10); by Lemma3.5, under strong
pseudo-regularity it is enough to check a uniform spectral gap for the unweighted
support graphBi​j:=𝟏{ℛi​j>0}B_{ij}:=\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathcal{R}_{ij}>0\}}. In particular, if all off-diagonal
entries ofℛ\mathcal{R}are positive and comparable, then the support graph is complete and
the second assertion of Lemma3.5gives (10).

## B.1.Proofs for ERGMs

In this subsection, we prove the Theorem2.1for edge-triangle ERGM and Theorem2.3for edge-three-star ERGM. Note that for Theorem2.1, it suffices to validate the conditions of the main results in Section1. For Theorem2.3, we will directly prove the general ill-conditioned results.

## B.1.1.Proofs for edge-triangle ERGM

The proof of Theorem2.1for the edge-triangle ERGM follows from the following two lemmas, which basically validate the conditions in the general results of Section1.

## Proof of Theorem2.1.

By LemmaB.1, the edge–triangle tensor satisfies Assumption1.2, the mean-field condition (4), asymptotic regularity (5), nontriviality (7), and the spectral gap condition (10). Therefore Theorem1.13applies under the assumptions in part (a) and givesTN​(X)=op​(1)T_{N}(X)=o_{p}(1).

For part (b), LemmaB.2verifies the variational gap hypothesis of Theorem1.11for every fixedh0h_{0}, providedβ0\beta_{0}is sufficiently negative. Thus Theorem1.11givesPβ0,h0​(TN​(X)⩾c)→1P_{\beta_{0},h_{0}}(T_{N}(X)\geqslant c)\to 1for somec>0c>0. Applying Theorem1.3then yields existence of the MPLE with probability tending to one and jointN\sqrt{N}-consistency for(β0,h0)(\beta_{0},h_{0}).■\blacksquare

independently ofee. Thus Assumption1.2and
(7) hold for all sufficiently largenn.

## B.2.Proofs for 3-term Arithmetic Progressions

We first introduce the following lemmas to validate the conditions of our general results.

## Lemma B.5.

For both the cyclic tensorAcycA^{\mathrm{cyc}}and the integer
tensorAintA^{\mathrm{int}}, Assumption1.2, the
nontriviality condition (7), and the strong
pseudo-regularity condition
(6) hold. Moreover,Tr​(ℛ2)=O​(1)\mathrm{Tr}(\mathcal{R}^{2})=O(1)and
the mean-field condition (4) follows.

In the cyclic case, the row sums are constant, and hence the
asymptotic regularity condition (5) holds. In the
integer case,∑i(ℛi−ℛ¯)2=Ω​(N).\sum_{i}(\mathcal{R}_{i}-\bar{\mathcal{R}})^{2}=\Omega(N).

## Proof.

For distincta,ba,b, letqa​b:=#​{c:{a,b,c}​forms a nontrivial3-AP}.q_{ab}:=\#\bigl\{c:\{a,b,c\}\text{ forms a nontrivial $3$-AP}\bigr\}.

Then, in either model,ℛa​b=∑cAa​b​c=qa​bN.\mathcal{R}_{ab}=\sum_{c}A_{abc}=\frac{q_{ab}}{N}.For a fixed paira,ba,b, the possible completions arise fromc=2​a−b,c=2​b−a,2​c=a+b.c=2a-b,\qquad c=2b-a,\qquad 2c=a+b.

In the integer model, the last equation has at most one solution,
soqa​b⩽3q_{ab}\leqslant 3. In the cyclic model, the equation2​c=a+b2c=a+bhas at most two solutions, soqa​b⩽4q_{ab}\leqslant 4. Therefore,
in both models,1N⩽ℛa​b⩽4Nwhenever​ℛa​b>0.\frac{1}{N}\leqslant\mathcal{R}_{ab}\leqslant\frac{4}{N}\qquad\text{whenever }\mathcal{R}_{ab}>0.

Thus the strong pseudo-regularity condition
(6) holds withK=4K=4. Also,ℛa=∑bℛa​b⩽4,\mathcal{R}_{a}=\sum_{b}\mathcal{R}_{ab}\leqslant 4,so Assumption1.2holds, for example withγ=4\gamma=4. Furthermore,Tr​(R2)=∑a,bRa​b2⩽N2​(4/N)2⩽16.\mathrm{Tr}(R^{2})=\sum_{a,b}R_{ab}^{2}\leqslant N^{2}(4/N)^{2}\leqslant 16.

We next verify nontriviality. In the cyclic model, translation
invariance shows thatℛa=rN\mathcal{R}_{a}=r_{N}is independent ofaa. For fixedaa, consider allbbsuch thatb≠ab\neq aand2​(a−b)≢0(modN)2(a-b)\not\equiv 0\pmod{N}. Settingc=2​a−bc=2a-b, the triple(b,a,c)(b,a,c)is a nontrivial cyclic33-AP. Henceℛa⩾N−2N⩾12forN⩾4.\mathcal{R}_{a}\geqslant\frac{N-2}{N}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{for $N\geqslant 4$}.

For the integer model, a direct count givesℛi=2N​(⌊i−12⌋+⌊N−i2⌋+min⁡{i−1,N−i}).\mathcal{R}_{i}=\frac{2}{N}\Big(\Big\lfloor\frac{i-1}{2}\Big\rfloor+\Big\lfloor\frac{N-i}{2}\Big\rfloor+\min\{i-1,N-i\}\Big).

In particular,ℛi⩾1/2\mathcal{R}_{i}\geqslant 1/2for everyiiand everyN⩾4N\geqslant 4.
Thus (7) holds in both models. SinceTr​(ℛ2)=O​(1)=o​(N/log⁡N),\mathrm{Tr}(\mathcal{R}^{2})=O(1)=o\left(N/\log N\right),Lemma3.4(b) yields the mean-field
condition (4).

In the cyclic case, translation invariance also shows that the
row sums are constant, so (5) holds. In the integer
case, uniformly forα\alphain compact subsets of(0,1)(0,1),ℛ⌊α​N⌋=1+2​min⁡{α,1−α}+O​(N−1).\mathcal{R}_{\lfloor\alpha N\rfloor}=1+2\min\{\alpha,1-\alpha\}+O(N^{-1}).

Since the limiting profile is nonconstant, a Riemann-sum argument
gives∑i(ℛi−ℛ¯)2=Ω​(N).\sum_{i}(\mathcal{R}_{i}-\bar{\mathcal{R}})^{2}=\Omega(N).■\blacksquare

## Lemma B.6.

For the cyclic33-AP tensorAcycA^{\mathrm{cyc}}, the spectral-gap
condition (10) holds.

## Proof.

LetBa​b:=𝟏{ℛa​b>0}B_{ab}:=\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathcal{R}_{ab}>0\}}be the support graph ofℛ\mathcal{R}in the
cyclic model. We first identify this graph. Ifa≠ba\neq band2​(a−b)≢0(modN)2(a-b)\not\equiv 0\pmod{N}, thenc=2​a−bc=2a-bis distinct from bothaaandbb,
so{b,a,c}\{b,a,c\}is a nontrivial cyclic 3-AP. ThusBa​b=1B_{ab}=1.

It remains only to consider the case2​(a−b)≡0(modN)2(a-b)\equiv 0\pmod{N}, which can occur only
whenNNis even andb=a+N/2b=a+N/2. IfN≡0(mod4)N\equiv 0\pmod{4}, then the equation2​c=a+b(modN)2c=a+b\pmod{N}has solutions, and any such solution is distinct froma,ba,b;
henceBa​b=1B_{ab}=1. IfN≡2(mod4)N\equiv 2\pmod{4}, then this equation has no solution, and
the two other possible completions givec=ac=aorc=bc=b. Hence in this case the
support graph is the complete graph with the perfect matching{a,a+N/2}\{a,a+N/2\}removed.

ThereforeBBis either the complete graph, or the complete graph with a perfect
matching removed. In both cases the simple random walk onBBhas1−λ2​(DB−1​B)⩾1.1-\lambda_{2}(D_{B}^{-1}B)\geqslant 1.Indeed, for the complete graph all nontrivial eigenvalues are−1/(N−1)-1/(N-1), while

is0.

By LemmaB.5, the strong pseudo-regularity condition holds withK=4K=4. Lemma3.5therefore gives1−λ2​(D−1​R)⩾14.1-\lambda_{2}(D^{-1}R)\geqslant\frac{1}{4}.This proves the spectral-gap condition (10).■\blacksquare

The next lemma supplies the additive-combinatorial input needed for showing variational gap for cyclic33-AP in the strong antiferromagnetic regime. It
is a special case of[SahSawhneyZhao21, Proposition 2.1], but we include a proof for completeness.

## Lemma B.7.

There exist constantsδ0,K>0\delta_{0},K>0such that, for every0<δ⩽δ00<\delta\leqslant\delta_{0}and every sufficiently largeN∈ℕN\in\mathbb{N}, there exists a subsetA⊆ℤ/N​ℤA\subseteq\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}with|A|⩾δ​N|A|\geqslant\delta Nsuch thatT3cyc​(A)⩽δK​log⁡(1/δ)​N2.T_{3}^{\mathrm{cyc}}(A)\leqslant\delta^{K\log(1/\delta)}N^{2}.

## Proof.

Fixc>0c>0from Behrend’s theorem, so that for every sufficiently large integerMMthere exists a setB⊆[M]B\subseteq[M]withT3int​(B)=0T_{3}^{\mathrm{int}}(B)=0and|B|⩾M​e−c​log⁡M|B|\geqslant Me^{-c\sqrt{\log M}}. Chooseδ0>0\delta_{0}>0so small that for every0<δ⩽δ00<\delta\leqslant\delta_{0}, the integerM:=⌊exp⁡((4​c2)−1​log2⁡(1/δ))⌋M:=\lfloor\exp((4c^{2})^{-1}\log^{2}(1/\delta))\rfloor

is large enough for Behrend’s theorem to apply and also satisfiesδ1/2⩾12​δ\delta^{1/2}\geqslant 12\delta. With this choice, we havee−c​log⁡M​M⩾δ1/2​M⩾12​δ​Me^{-c\sqrt{\log M}}M\geqslant\delta^{1/2}M\geqslant 12\delta M. Hence there exists a setB⊆[M]B\subseteq[M]such thatT3int​(B)=0and|B|⩾12​δ​M.T_{3}^{\mathrm{int}}(B)=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad|B|\geqslant 12\delta M.

Also,M⩾δ−C0​log⁡(1/δ)M\geqslant\delta^{-C_{0}\log(1/\delta)}for some absolute constantC0>0C_{0}>0.
SetL:=3​ML:=3M,m:=⌊N/(6​M)⌋m:=\lfloor N/(6M)\rfloor, andA:=⋃r=0m−1(r​L+B)⊆ℤ/N​ℤ.A:=\bigcup_{r=0}^{m-1}(rL+B)\subseteq\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}.

SinceB⊆[M]B\subseteq[M], the translatesr​L+BrL+Bare disjoint. For part (b), LemmasB.9andB.10show that, withℙA\mathbb{P}_{A}-probability tending to one, the tensor satisfies Assumption1.2, the mean-field condition
(4), asymptotic regularity (5), nontriviality (7),

## Appendix CProofs for Auxiliary lemmas

## Proof of Lemma3.6.

LetX∼μX\sim\mu. Suppose forc∈{κ−,κ+}c\in\{\kappa_{-},\kappa_{+}\}, we haveP​(X=c)>0P(X=c)>0. Thus for anyλ∈ℝ\lambda\in\mathbb{R}, we have𝔼⁡eλ​X⩾eλ​c​P​(X=c)\operatorname{\mathds{E}}e^{\lambda X}\geqslant e^{\lambda c}P(X=c).
Takinglog\logon both sides, it givesΛ​(λ)⩾λ​c+log⁡P​(X=c).\Lambda(\lambda)\geqslant\lambda c+\log P(X=c).Equivalentlyλ​c−Λ​(λ)⩽−log⁡P​(X=c)<∞\lambda c-\Lambda(\lambda)\leqslant-\log P(X=c)<\infty. This shows that if the boundary pointsκ−,κ+\kappa_{-},\kappa_{+}have positive mass, thenI​(κ−),I​(κ+)<∞I(\kappa_{-}),I(\kappa_{+})<\infty. Now we turn to the interior part.

Sinceκ−,κ+∈supp​(μ)\kappa_{-},\kappa_{+}\in\text{supp}(\mu), we havelimλ→∞Λ​(λ)λ=κ+andlimλ→−∞Λ​(λ)λ=κ−.\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}\frac{\Lambda(\lambda)}{\lambda}=\kappa_{+}\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{\lambda\to-\infty}\frac{\Lambda(\lambda)}{\lambda}=\kappa_{-}.

It implies that for eachx∈(κ−,κ+)x\in(\kappa_{-},\kappa_{+}), there exist aλ0>0\lambda_{0}>0such thatλ​x−Λ​(λ)=λ​(x−Λ​(λ)λ)<0for all|λ|⩾λ0.\lambda x-\Lambda(\lambda)=\lambda\Big(x-\frac{\Lambda(\lambda)}{\lambda}\Big)<0\quad\text{for all $\left|\lambda\right|\geqslant\lambda_{0}$}.

Since the functionλ↦λ​x−Λ​(λ)\lambda\mapsto\lambda x-\Lambda(\lambda)is continuous, we havesup|λ|⩽λ0(λ​x−Λ​(λ))<∞\sup_{\left|\lambda\right|\leqslant\lambda_{0}}(\lambda x-\Lambda(\lambda))<\infty. Therefore, we have shown that for eachx∈(κ−,κ+)x\in(\kappa_{-},\kappa_{+}),I​(x)<∞I(x)<\infty. The proof is complete.■\blacksquare

## Proof of Lemma3.7.

Letκ¯=κ−+κ+2\bar{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa_{-}+\kappa_{+}}{2}, fixδ>0\delta>0and setL=⌈κ+−κ−δ⌉,𝒟N​(δ):={a​𝟏:a∈{κ¯,κ¯±δ2,κ¯±δ,⋯,κ¯±L​δ2}}.L=\big\lceil\frac{\kappa_{+}-\kappa_{-}}{\delta}\big\rceil,\qquad\mathcal{D}_{N}(\delta):=\Big\{a{\bf 1}:a\in\Big\{\bar{\kappa},\bar{\kappa}\pm\frac{\delta}{2},\bar{\kappa}\pm\delta,\cdots,\bar{\kappa}\pm L\frac{\delta}{2}\Big\}\Big\}.

Then|𝒟N​(δ)|=2​L+1|\mathcal{D}_{N}(\delta)|=2L+1, which is free ofNN, and only depends onδ\delta. Finally, for anyy∈ENy\in E_{N}, leta∈{κ¯,κ¯±δ2,κ¯±δ,⋯,κ¯±L​δ2}a\in\Big\{\bar{\kappa},\bar{\kappa}\pm\frac{\delta}{2},\bar{\kappa}\pm\delta,\cdots,\bar{\kappa}\pm L\frac{\delta}{2}\Big\}be such that|a−y¯|⩽δ2|a-\bar{y}|\leqslant\frac{\delta}{2}. Then we have∑i(yi−a)2=∑i(yi−y¯)2+N​(y¯−a)2⩽N​εN+N​δ24⩽N​δ2,\displaystyle\sum_{i}(y_{i}-a)^{2}=\sum_{i}(y_{i}-\bar{y})^{2}+N(\bar{y}-a)^{2}\leqslant N\varepsilon_{N}+N\frac{\delta^{2}}{4}\leqslant N\delta^{2},

where the last inequality holds for allNNlarge enough (depending onδ>0\delta>0). Thus we have shown that for allδ>0\delta>0, the set𝒟N​(δ)\mathcal{D}_{N}(\delta)is aδ​N\delta\sqrt{N}net forENE_{N}in Euclidean metric with bounded size, and soENE_{N}is of low complexity, as desired.■\blacksquare

## Proof of Lemma3.8.

By a direct calculation, the RHS equals13​(a3+b3+c3)−13​(a3/2​b3/2+b3/2​c3/2+c3/2​a3/2).\tfrac{1}{3}\big(a^{3}+b^{3}+c^{3}\big)-\tfrac{1}{3}\big(a^{3/2}b^{3/2}+b^{3/2}c^{3/2}+c^{3/2}a^{3/2}\big).

By the AM-GM inequality, we have13​(a3/2​b3/2+b3/2​c3/2+c3/2​a3/2)⩾(a3​b3​c3)1/3=a​b​c,\tfrac{1}{3}\big(a^{3/2}b^{3/2}+b^{3/2}c^{3/2}+c^{3/2}a^{3/2}\big)\geqslant(a^{3}b^{3}c^{3})^{1/3}=abc,

and the proof follows.■\blacksquare

## Proof of Lemma3.9.

LetYYbe a random variable such thatℙ​(Y=yi)=N−1\mathbb{P}(Y=y_{i})=N^{-1}for allii. DefineZ=YcZ=Y^{c}. To prove the lemma, we need to show thatVar​(Y)⩽Var​(Z)1/c.\mathrm{Var}(Y)\leqslant\mathrm{Var}(Z)^{1/c}.LetY1Y_{1}andZ1Z_{1}be independent copy ofYYandZZrespectively. We writeVar(Y)=12𝔼(Y−Y1)2=12𝔼(Z1/c−Z11/c)2⩽12𝔼[(Z−Z1)2/c],\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(Y)=\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{\mathds{E}}(Y-Y_{1})^{2}=\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{\mathds{E}}\big(Z^{1/c}-Z_{1}^{1/c}\big)^{2}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{\mathds{E}}[(Z-Z_{1})^{2/c}],

where the last step uses the inequality|a1/c−b1/c|⩽|a−b|1/c|a^{1/c}-b^{1/c}|\leqslant|a-b|^{1/c}for anya,b⩾0,a,b\geqslant 0,which is a simple consequence of the concavity oft↦t1/c.t\mapsto t^{1/c}.Sincec>1,c>1,by Jensen’s inequality, it follows that12​𝔼⁡[(Z−Z1)2/c]⩽12​(𝔼⁡[(Z−Z1)2])1/c=12​(2​Var​(Z))1/c⩽Var​(Z)1/c.\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{\mathds{E}}[(Z-Z_{1})^{2/c}]\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\operatorname{\mathds{E}}[(Z-Z_{1})^{2}]\big)^{1/c}=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(2\ \mathrm{Var}(Z)\big)^{1/c}\leqslant\mathrm{Var}(Z)^{1/c}.

■\blacksquare
